Or another example: We've already talked about the Rolling Landstraße today. This is the so-called huckepack traffic, which is particularly important for smaller companies that are less able to travel with unaccompanied combined transport, i.e. they do not have containers. They ask themselves: Why is it not possible to load at the border, why do all trucks have to drive on the motorways until they arrive at the transhipment points, the terminals in the middle of the federal states or in the middle of the country? It is a very interesting project in Upper Austria in preparation, where we can achieve a lot with minor measures. These are not the billions that we need for the railway infrastructure, but they are also not insignificant sizes, although for my area around 100 million shillings are relatively small, but they can be used very efficiently. We can bring a place of loading directly to the border, only need a place of unloading and a connecting track, and then it is possible to bring up to and write 500 trucks from the road to the rail. What discharge for the A 8 is, you can imagine. – This is just an example. I am currently in the process of extending the RoLa at the same time as I am in the area of the Czech Republic, the Summerau Railway, to strengthen and attract this route. In this way, action will be taken consecutively, one project after another will be carried out, and at the same time the priorities will be set in development. Then: The West Railway has high priority.

ParlaMint-AT_2001-07-05-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00075_d7e12366	Federal Chancellor! Mrs. Vice chancellor! Mrs. Federal Minister! High house! The new university service law is on the agenda, but even if we are talking about university service law today and will subsequently also vote on it, we cannot separate this subject from the environment in which this discussion takes place today. It is not possible to separate the new university service law from the attempt to take over power in the social security of workers and employees. [[Abg. Dr. Brinek: We are at another item on the agenda!]] This debate cannot be separated from an attempt to influence the largest media organ in this country, as we have just experienced. You can't separate this from the environment of political development towards privatization and the relationship between state and private in the education system. In the preliminary discussions and discussions in the run-up to this university service law, it was just argued by the government parties of the public in a way that the new university service law is a kind of displacement of the members of the universities from a paradise – a necessary expulsion, because it is so insidiously good or too good for all of them and because there is something to change in this regard. We would like to thank those who argue in this way for the evidence that, under the SPÖ-led government, workers at universities have been better off – especially since you have the impression that they might have gone too well. We are not ashamed that we have provided a service right to ensure that people can work properly and that they find good conditions. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] In the run-up to the discussion, the opinion of Professor Marhold, who made a European legal comparison, was also cited again and again, and then argued that according to this opinion, there was only more such a right of service in the Czech Republic than in our case. I have looked at this very carefully, and it is worth looking at this comparative study, for which we thank the Ministry. Professor Marhold's central thesis is that it depends on the entire labour law environment in a state, such as public service law, and that there is a greater level of protection for university teachers, where general labour law gives workers such increased protection. What do I want to say? – This means that Austria has a high level of protection over all decades of social-democratic social policy in relation to labour rights and, of course, it also affects universities. If you go here now and say that this is to be changed and you want to start with it first in public service law, then we know what the result will be: These things are not separate, and this will therefore be equally true of the protection and rights of workers and employees in the other areas of work. It is therefore necessary to say that there is a need for caution when it comes to the dismantling of such rights. The standing ovations, which can also be expected at the end of this item on the agenda – because you complain of every law, although it is somehow natural that one can decide such a thing by a majority – the congratulations that you express yourself are not decisive for us, but is decisive for us, what those concerned say about such a law. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] I'm allowed to bring a few quotes to that. ==References====External links== July 2001, from the Vienna University of Economics, which is certainly a university, is open to modern developments: "The planned novelle does not make a suitable arrangement for the appointment of replacement forces for the duration of carnations..." and so on. – Nothing has been changed! It also says: "The proposed scheme prevents international research visits by younger scientists and acts discriminatoryly for women." You have got it yourself, Mr Grollitsch, and I hope you have read it through so that I do not have to read the explanatory statement here again. You know what the universities say about these things. Or the Johannes Kepler University of Linz, also one of our reforming universities: "The Senate of the Johannes Kepler University of Linz points to the expected catastrophic effects of the new service law." – This is done even more precisely here as a result. I would also like to mention the University Teaching Assembly or the Senate of the University of Innsbruck, which rejects the concept as completely missing, and which is also detailed. This goes down to the Austrian Medical Chamber, which draws attention to the fact that specialist training is at risk because what is to happen to these people is by no means appropriate. – These are some quotations from those concerned, and they should give us some thought. Let me also present two main arguments for our rejection. First of all, this is a matter of substance, which is the subject of funding. We are well aware that there is a motion for a resolution which seeks to establish once again what has been negotiated, because this outcome of the negotiations has now been abandoned by the government. Conversion of public service relations into private law costs something. It is generally known that these transformations, at least in the first stages, are rather expensive. It is not enough for us that a resolution says that the Minister of Finance must pay this. We've got the latte on 30. It was so consciously laid in May, that we said: If there are legal changes here that expressly guarantee the financing, then that would be all right. A motion for a resolution on this is not sufficient for us. A second thing we have always pointed out, also at the university survey: Time-limits of a general nature can be imagined for top positions. But a company that, as a service provider, says most of its staff: You're getting busy for four years now and then again for four or six years, and then this service relationship automatically ends, whether you're good or bad, whether qualified, whether diligent or not, it definitely ends up!, that's a bad job, and he doesn't get the best people either. Let me bring you a very up-to-date quote from Daniel Goeuvert, the book published in his book today "The horizon has wings. The future of education" reports, among other things, a conversation he has conducted with Bill Gates – I quote this literally, because this is a very central passage –: "It was only recently that Bill Gates told me on the verge of an event that he had reintroduced new hierarchy levels and long-term employment contracts in his company. Like so many others, he had followed the recommendations of the management guru in the 1990s, the company hierarchy had set in and encouraged his employees to mobility. The result was a real disappointment: Many of his best executives turned their backs on Microsoft due to lack of ascent opportunities and let themselves be recruited from the competition. Other employees lost their motivation because of the mobility issue and felt, as it were, constantly on the jump into a professional alternative. The Group thereby suffered damage..." We have not joined the argument of this gurus, and we believe that universities must try to get the best scientists, and that the right to serve must also be a prerequisite. Because it does not, we reject this draft.

ParlaMint-AT_2001-07-05-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00075_d7e19092	Mr President. High house! Ladies and gentlemen! The focus of the reform measures in the university area is on increasing the international competitiveness of our universities. In this context, it is also essential to adapt the service provisions to the conditions of competition in the European area, in particular the EU Member States, but also to the future candidate countries for the forthcoming enlargement. In the course of the redesign of the university teacher's service rights, consideration is also given to the existing regulations in other European countries. None of these systems provides for an undetermined service procedure for assistants. To that end, I must also correct colleagues of Niederwieser, who meant that international comparisons would confirm his opinion. Don't do that! International comparisons confirm that a university teacher service law must be modern, performance-oriented and contractual in order to safeguard and enhance the attractiveness of the economic location in order to improve the opportunities for young academics to pursue a scientific or artistic career. The transition between the university and the private sector, the mobility between these areas, must be promoted. It is particularly important in the field of university and non-university research that there is mutual exchange here and that universities be able to renew the scientific staff within a manageable period. These objectives are not attainable by current law of service and, above all, the access of young people to university careers would be increasingly narrow and finally completely closed. That is what Dr. Grünewald has addressed, and I am sorry that I have to object to him. The fact is that today we have a situation where young people, young academics hardly have the opportunity or the opportunity to enter a university career at all. Since you will not believe this, I would also like to quote some unwarranted witnesses who have dealt with the previous career model at the universities. The "standard" has recently written: "Did the ruling career model in high schools so far prefer grey mice? Some people talk about it. For example: Those who do well to move away from the university feeder and, as a small assistant as long as possible, have skinned his’ professor on their stomachs, were significantly more often rewarded with higher academic consecrations than others. ... In recent years, all the assistants have been pramatized. Many subjects are more or less concrete." - Quote. And that's exactly the problem. We had the situation that those who showed mobility that went abroad, or even women who just took a family break, who dared to do so, in the closed university landscape, often could not take the right steps again. That is why this new university teacher service law is so important with a four-stage career model, a model that is characterized by the fact that admission in each individual stage has to lead to an application or evaluation and that direct entry into each stage should be possible. However, through the application system and thus quality confirmation, it is also possible to obtain a continuous university career – contrary to some claims made in recent weeks. All inclusive fees are intended to make it much better to match the functionality of the individual jobs in the structure of the university business than has been the case so far. This will also enable universities to develop more personal responsibility for personnel management and to cover more flexible staff needs than previously. This is also important for the competitiveness of top universities. Dr. Grünewald! It is polemic to compare Stanford and Klagenfurt with each other, but the fact is that we should strive for the best possible standard for our universities, and that also means that we should have the best possible choice among university teachers. It's by far not the way you said it was. [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] I also believe that it speaks for itself that, although we have reached a consensus in very long and difficult negotiations with the university teachers' union, we have reached a consensus. That is why I do not fully understand the SPÖ's attitude, the attitude of Mr Niederweiser. Mr Niederweiser, your statement was as follows: When a consensus is reached with the trade union – and this is legitimate that You have made this a condition – then you could agree to this law as well. Well: The union has agreed, and you have done very hard – you have seen it again today – to find some explanation that you want to reject this university teacher service right. – There is no reason because this right of service has a model character for the entire public service in Austria. I also thank the trade union for the possibility of adopting this right of service in consensus. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] However, the Novelle Service Law today for decision-making also includes a number of other important areas for other public service professions. There are a number of provisions, some of which I would like to take out, namely those relating to the field of executives. The recent events in Salzburg, which have been discussed in connection with the urgent question here in this House, have shown that the best possible protection of executive officials who are exposed to particularly difficult situations, who are exposed to aggression, who are subjected to violence by demonstrators, and who are also supposed to ensure and have to secure adequate back cover from their service providers, that is, the Republic. That was also our pursuit within the framework of this Novelle Service Law. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] One of the essential points is that we are now providing for a possibility to advance pain money, because we believe that it is not reasonable for an official that, if he has been given justice in court and has been granted pain money and then it is not or only difficult to bring it in, so to speak, at his own risk. That is why we have made sure that there is a corresponding advance of this pain. [[New congratulations to the liberals and the ÖVP.]] In addition, it has shown a current occasion that in the current form, the Guard Service Services Act is not sufficiently determined by law, in order to provide the necessary protection and assistance for the individual officials and their families. We have seen that the existing legal provisions would not have been sufficient to give these officials and their families the necessary assistance. Therefore, it was important for me to amend this accordingly, so that there is also clarity that the intentions of the law can be implemented in practical implementation. We have now made this law so clear that any civil servant who falls into service or comes to harm can be sure that he is also receiving a corresponding assistance under this law. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] In addition, we have also ensured a system that has been overdue for a long time. In connection with court charges of executive officials who have to answer before the court, it has now been ensured that, in case of an acquittal of time, charges of witnesses or accused charges are also compensated retroactively with leisure time in relation to 1 : 1. This too was a great nuisance in the executive, especially when the people had to take long directions and all of this happened in leisure time. I believe that these improvements are necessary and correct, especially for a professional group that has an extremely difficult service to perform. We have seen in recent days, on the weekend in Salzburg, but also today in Vienna, that these officials are faced with very special challenges, which they face with great brawl, with great discretion and with great responsibility, and for this they also deserve our thanks, our recognition and our respect.

Even the sometimes inconspicuous criticism and opposition policy in the field of science will not change anything about it and will not dispel us from our path.

ParlaMint-AT_2001-07-05-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00075_d7e21929	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! An efficient and stable financial market is of insufficient importance for the entire economy of a country, both in terms of stability and in terms of the economy and its growth. The changed framework conditions in the financial sector, including through European integration, changes in the financing behaviour of companies and the internationally visible trend of an increasingly cross-sectoral supply of financial services by individual financial institutions, require a serious rethinking in the framework of financial supervision. In one word: There is a need for action. In principle, we Socialists were and are in favour of the reform idea of financial market supervision and the concept of pan-financial supervision. We believe that such a reform must be aimed at achieving a high-quality, effective, forward-looking and cost-effective form of such financial market supervision. It is therefore obvious that such an institution, if it is to be internationally accepted, must be politically independent and free of any direction. What is the opinion of the Austrian National Bank on the draft government? – On the part of the Austrian National Bank, there is also no compelling need to create an all-financial supervisory authority in view of the structure of the Austrian financial centre. However, if, despite the above statements of the political will, a comprehensive supervisory solution is preferable, it must be explicitly stressed that a financial market surveillance authority only complies with the basic principles of the Basel Banking Supervisory Committee or the IMF's ideas if it is made independent and non-dispute. ParlaMint-AT_2001-07-05-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00075_d7e29452	Mr President. Federal Minister! Mr State Secretary! My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! The need for reform of banking supervision has been on the political agenda for a number of years. The need for reform is due to several factors: Once that the international standards have developed further and that what we currently have in Austria, namely banking supervision within the framework of the Ministry of Finance, is actually an Austrian cum. Internationally, it is absolutely clear that banking supervision must be operationally independent. The second is trends in the financial world, which are clearly heading towards all-financial corporations. That means as you all know: Insurances shall establish or buy banks; Insurances have pension funds or not. This means that today’s Kasterl thinking – banking supervision, insurance supervision, securities supervision, pension fund supervision – is obsolete and no longer time-adequate. The third thing is: As you can certainly remember, in April of this year there was a massive criticism of the Court of Auditors, which cumulated in the sentence: The Court of Auditors cannot rule out a case such as the Rieger bank once again occurs if we do not implement reforms here. The need for reform was thus given. I very much regret that this reform was not possible under the previous political constellation. We have already tried to make such a reform at the time. The fact that this great reform work is now in place is only another proof that this government constellation was necessary to carry out reforms in Austria. This is also the case in the European Area applies to "Rerule Austria": We finally have a reform in the field of financial market supervision! Ladies and gentlemen! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] I think we should also say very clearly: Not only do we need to comply with international standards, but it also has an immense interest in the local savers and the local investors that cases such as those of Bank Burgenland, Rieger Bank or Trigon Bank will no longer occur if possible. That was actually the core of all our efforts to create a banking supervision, an all-financial supervision that exploits, on the one hand, the most organisational synergies possible by combining all four areas mentioned under one roof, but that also gives more efficiency to banking supervision or financial supervision, as it is now called. I just mention a few examples: The qualifications requirements for the bank auditor have been significantly increased. It became a rotation of the bank auditors, it was also introduced external rotation. We have strengthened the supervisory rights of the Supervisory Board accordingly. – A large number of measures have been put in place, both organisational and material, to create an internationally recognizable, operationally independent financial market supervision with more "up to" and more efficiency. We have also put great emphasis on the close cooperation with the Austrian central bank, which, for example, is expressed in the fact that the two board members of the Executive Board can nominate one from the central bank, that three of the six Supervisory Board members can be nominated by the National Bank, by the central bank, that a financial market committee is legally established, in which, in addition to the Ministry of Finance and Financial Market Supervision, the central bank is also represented. Thus, maximum involvement is made of the proven human resources, know-how and expertise of the central bank. We have also explicitly stated in the Committee that the Committee assumes that on-the-spot checks in the field of market risk, in the area of credit risk on the basis of these human resources, on the basis of this expertise, are to be carried out by the central bank on the basis of this know-how of the staff of the central bank. There should therefore also be close cooperation with the central bank in this area. As far as the proposal by my previous speaker Kurt Heindl is concerned, I should like to say: I have rarely experienced the fact that a legislative work, a reform work has been prepared as professionally and thoroughly as this reform work. ParlaMint-AT_2001-07-05-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00075_d7e29805	Mr President, I should like to thank the President-in-Office of the Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen. Mr State Secretary! I may, because the starting position has been questioned, try to make a little effort in the history too, and I would like to point out the following things: There has been a debate on banking supervision over the last few decades. I am allowed to start with my predecessor, Federal Minister Dr. Androsch, during his term of office, in the context of the creation of an Austrian credit law in 1979, when it was said that there is a need for a reform of banking supervision. In the course of the Landesbank crisis, Bruno Kreisky, then Chancellor of the Federal Republic of Germany, pleaded for a strengthening of supervision, with the result that there were two new employees, but in reality there was no improvement of supervision under the then competent Minister Dr. Salcher. In the course of the Novelle Credit Act 1986, there have been numerous proposals for strengthening supervision, especially at civil servants level. Unfortunately, they were not realized by God, not even in consideration. Since 1986, there has essentially been no transfer of competence from the Federal Ministry of Finance, no improvement in supervision, but there has de facto been a shift in the field of on-the-spot testing and activity to the central bank. There have been supervisory discussions on specific occasions, which have also been mentioned, in greater numbers, for example in 1993 on the occasion of the crisis of the banking house Rössler, the securities bank number one. At BAWAG in 1994 in connection with Caribbean operations under then Minister Lacina pledged to tighten the supervision of the Bank for Trade and Industry in 1995 under the then Federal Minister Staribacher. There was an agreement between Federal Minister Edlinger and Federal Minister Molterer in February 1998, and they said: Some of us are trying to make an international comparison on supervisory structures, and we are trying to develop possible performance indicators for banking supervision. The aim was to intensify the construction of new banking supervision. Result: In June 1999, the supervisory reform failed again and no reform was made. That is why, ladies and gentlemen, I am delighted – and I say quite openly – that there will be a decision for financial market supervision today, because it shows that this federal government is developing solutions to long-standing problems, because it shows that we are consistently implementing the government agreement, and because it shows that we will achieve a quantum leap in financial supervision, which will lead Austria internationally to a model position, which will shape a trend and achieve a substantial improvement in the Austrian financial area. [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] At the same time, we are ending an unacceptable state of organisational fragmentation of financial supervision in several areas, which were also detrimental to the quality of financial supervision in Austria. I should like to mention once again: Banking supervision as well as insurance supervision and pension fund supervision are now formally subject to the Federal Ministry of Finance. In part it is perceived, but de facto it is carried out in the Austrian central bank. The Securities Supervisory Board is located in its own distributed authority. All of this is now being brought together in a new pan-financial supervision, which will be a public-law institution and a new seal of quality for the Austrian financial centre, a seal of quality, ladies and gentlemen, which allows several advantages, several strengths compared to the previous status quo: Firstly, there will be a concentration of procedural competence, enforcement competence and administrative responsibility in an authority, which will enable a substantial increase in the authority and enforcement capacity of that authority. Second point: We will be able to create synergies, because we have broken things apart today, we will be able to bring them together in an authority, and because at the same time we will take account of the economic developments that take place in practice, namely an increasing link to all-financial corporations, banks, insurances, pension funds, and Austria will not be lagging behind the actual developments. We're one step ahead. We are rethinking the future with this financial market supervision, ladies and gentlemen. [[Congratulations with the liberals and representatives of the ÖVP.]] Third point: It will be a public-law institution. I would like to stress this because this is a fundamental difference to the amendment tabled by the Socialists. We see a public-law institution and not a capital company which, of course, has a limited liability with the parent capital. I therefore think a public-law institution is necessary because we are speaking here of a core task of the state, a core task of the Republic of Austria in the interests of the savers, in the interests of investors. There is thus an official, stately nature, and every person concerned, every investor sees: It is the whole power of the State behind it to control and ensure appropriate control in the financial market. At the same time, we have created the flexibility of a legally independent institution, because we are no longer active in the ministry itself, but in an independent legal unit, which means that we will have quite, quite different possibilities in the personnel sector, especially, than we have before. Fourth point: We are fulfilling the basic principles with this construction, and we are creating an organisational, a budgetary, a human and an operational political independence of this authority, which is a very important progress in comparison to the status quo. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] Fifth, you open a bottle We have achieved close cooperation, in the final consequence, a common wearing of this pan-financial supervision, on the one hand through the Federal Ministry of Finance, which has formal competence, and on the other hand through the Austrian central bank. This is not the last thing we have done, because the essential points that have been raised by the Green Group and the Socialists in the many debates we have held have been brought in. It was important for us to take that up and try to achieve a good common solution. With this joint solution – which has been boldly presented by Member States: the Board of Directors will be jointly appointed by the central government and the Federal Government and the Federal Presidents; the Supervisory Board will be jointly appointed – it will be achieved that the strategic system supervision on the one hand and the more operational financial supervision in the narrower sense are linked to the individual institutions and that there will be a cooperation between the OeNB, the Federal Ministry of Finance and Financial Market Supervision, both in relation to system supervision and in terms of operational supervision in the narrower sense. Sixth point: We have also achieved significant improvements in material supervisory law and can thus achieve a substantial improvement in total in a first comprehensive step today. This first step will certainly follow a second step when it comes to implementing Basel II in the financial market surveillance sector in Austria, and if, of course, we can talk about things that have been stimulated today – for example, state commissioners – after a trial. Ladies and gentlemen! Material improvements will be made by the fact that we have significantly improved the requirements of bank auditors as regards their qualifications and reliability, as well as by providing for a rotation principle for auditors and companies every six years, and also by substantially strengthening the position of supervisory boards of credit institutions, because they can order auditors, because the Supervisory Board is regularly presented with internal revision reports, and because substantial improvements are provided in the Commercial Code, in the GmbH Act, in the Stock Act, in the direction of a stricter regulation of the liability of auditors. I think it is wise, ladies and gentlemen, that a auditor who gives his testate under such an examination is also liable accordingly that he can be held responsible, as is the case in the economy. This is an essential step that could be achieved – including with the support of the Minister of Justice – and I believe that this is an important point of this overall financial supervision. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] We have provided for more stringent penalties for the violation of information obligations, and we have not least provided for a change in the National Bank Act, which finally introduces payment system supervision in Austria. In total, ladies and gentlemen: For a year, we have really been working very consistently with all the preparations – with Professor Zechner, an international team of professors, who has been asked to present this international comparison, so that we can also achieve an internationally recognizable model solution – on this reform. We had eight presentations and rounds of negotiations with all the political groups in this House, because it was important for us to be very open here, quite transparent. We have also included amendments that have been tabled, because I too welcome it – Mr Kogler has referred to it; a committee statement on this is not at all a question; I also stressed this in the Committee – that we are not building double tracks here, but that we are, of course, concerned with the greatest possible efficiency and effectiveness and the cooperation between the central bank and financial market supervision. This, I believe, is not least the reason why the Austrian central bank also agrees to this solution and supports it and why, in the last episode, banks have also said: We'll carry that. This is an important step that is being taken here. We have therefore wanted and actively operated these many, many hours of discussions, because we have said: Here is not a place for party politics, but we want to achieve the best solution for Austria, for the Austrian financial centre. ParlaMint-AT_2001-07-05-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00075_d7e31021	Mr President. Federal Minister! Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! I believe that the subject of financial supervision has been discussed more or less emotionally and more or less in detail by my previous speakers, but in any case it has already been discussed in detail. I therefore agree to be brief in view of the advanced time and the agenda to be dealt with, and I would like to address only two points. First: The explanatory notes on the Government template in the form. XVI to § 275 HGB have created a certain legal uncertainty. However, the legislative text is clear and excludes liability of the auditor to third parties not mentioned in the law. The explanations wanted to leave the question of third party liability open. That is why we have adopted a committee in the Financial Committee to define the liability rules for auditors. Since the explanatory remarks only wanted to inform that international and national legal developments on the issue of statutory liability for auditors have not yet been completed, we have made it clear in the Financial Committee that according to the legal situation of § 275 HGB to be created by the Financial Market Supervision Act, the auditor's liability is final. Third parties not mentioned in the law are therefore not entitled to claim. Secondly, I would like to reject Mr Bauer's statement that the amendments relating to state commissioners are intended to involve additional members in the indebted circle of state commissioners with all the strictness. Both government parties assume that we will evaluate this situation once again after the operation of the Financial Supervision, and we are actually sure that we will be able to abolish the institution of the State Commissioners in about a year. The state commissioners have not been able to prevent the malaises, for example at the BHI, at Riegerbank and, of course, at the Bank Burgenland, nor have they been able to indicate them in good time. That is why I believe that we can say with fug and right that this institution would be abolished in the medium term. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2001-07-05-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00075_d7e31077	Mr President. High house! It is undisputed – and this has also emerged from the discussion – that there was an urgent need for action. However, it is also undisputed in science and teaching that supervision of money and credit is a central task of the state. There can be no solution, therefore, according to which supervision of money and credit is taken entirely out of the hands of the State, for there can be no ministerial responsibility on the one hand if the Minister on the other hand has no influence on the appointment of bodies, legal design and implementation at all. However, what is necessary and what the original draft has provided for is the functionally independent supervision of money and credit, with a certain possibility of guidance. That was the good thing about the new regulation. Unfortunately, it has not succeeded, but the solution presented today in an amendment or also in the discussions is not possible for several reasons. As I said, There can be no solution according to which supervision is entirely independent of ministerial responsibility, and there can be no solution according to which the audited persons themselves are represented in the supervisory body: In the National Bank, the banks are represented in the institutions, and in the case of a subsidiary, the auditors would have a supervisory or regulatory possibility. When additional costs arise, the new requirements already mentioned, namely the Basler decisions. But these additional costs would also have been incurred in the existing scheme. Therefore, the argument is wrong that a new, expensive authority is being created here. In accordance with the proposal in today's amendment tabled by the Social Democrats, a scheme is provided for under which a new company as a subsidiary of the National Bank is to receive a contract such as insurance supervision and supervision of pension funds, an activity which it has never been involved with, and which is contrary to the interests of the banks represented there. Professor Zechner had the task of looking for all international solutions and examining where the trend is going. He had the task to face the advantages and disadvantages of all variants. In the opinion also presented to the financial committee members, this was discussed carefully and all advantages and disadvantages were analysed. In principle, it was agreed with all parties that a new regulation on financial market supervision needs to be created in line with current and future requirements. However, one was already in agreement on the legal form of this FMA: If FPÖ and ÖVP were to present the proposal on the formation of a new, non-wisdom FMA in the form of a public-law institution, the SPÖ wanted to present itself as a subsidiary of the OeNB in a 100% ownership of the OeNB. Another reason why the SPÖ refuses to approve is the different view as to the location of the audit bodies. The FPÖ/ÖVP government considers that on-the-spot inspections of OeNB representatives could be carried out. The SPÖ considered that these credit risk and market risk assessments should be carried out exclusively by the OeNB. The rotation system can be described as a further positive detail for this new FMA in external testers. From 2004, these audit companies will be exchanged every six years. The costs incurred by the supervisor cannot, of course, be carried out without discussion. And if development cooperation is to be effective, there must also be debt models. This has even been recognised by the World Bank and Monetary Fund, and since 1996 there has been the so-called HIPC initiative for the most indebted poor countries. Many of the industrialized countries have already contributed to the Trust Fund, Austria has not yet done so. I very much welcome the fact that we are going to decide today to make a contribution from Austria to this trust fund, because this is one of the necessary measures and a first step towards recognizing that, as far as this debt spiral is concerned, which is still aggravating poverty in the poorest countries, which really makes the situation of many people there impossible and endangers existence, something has to happen, that sustainable development is only possible with a lasting solution to this over-indebtedness problem. Without this solution, sustainability, whether ecological or social or economic, cannot be discussed. This is therefore an important first step, which we welcome too, but Austria should simply not stand, but continue, in fulfilling this minimum consensus within the framework of this HIPC initiative. And if you ask yourself: How?, our request, which is available to the Financial Committee, would have provided you with good opportunities. There is – our request has been addressed – an excellent book by Martina Neuwirth and Barbara Rohregger, "Future without debt", which has drawn up very concrete alternatives and proposals, which would, for example, mean that Austria should simply remove the debt of the poorest countries, which are simply priceless. That's where you can say: This cannot be done, they have just been indebted!, but if you look at it carefully and evaluate what the administrative costs of re-indebtedness cost, then you might realize that it makes sense simply to draw a final line here and give these countries the opportunity to develop in a direction that does not mean any increased poverty. With this study there are sophisticated proposals, for example the idea of counterpart funds, as they already exist in Switzerland. Unfortunately, the Financial Committee did not agree to these proposals. We then had the opportunity to talk to you about this in the subcommittee on development policy, Mr State Secretary, and I am glad that there was the agreement that the authors of this study should also be invited to the working group of the Ministry of Finance and the Ministry of Foreign Affairs to discuss with them in the future. I hope and am also convinced that you, Mr State Secretary, if you really want to consider this debt, as you are now trying it in Austria – but this is another subject – as something important, then you also want to take seriously proposals that are contained in our request and in this book. Of the world's 2 465 billion dollar debt, the poorest countries, the HIPC countries, have to carry 71 billion US dollars. It is an overwhelming debt burden, which is mostly bloated by interest and interest service. We all know the pictures from African countries, and we know how the people there suffer. Willy Brandt once said the debt problem and the interest and the Interest service is like blood transfusion from the sick to healthy. For the World Bank initiative, which is working to advance the halvion of poverty by 2015, a basic condition is debt. The HIPC is financed by gold sales from the International Monetary Fund, from bilateral contributions, which unfortunately come only very hesitant – Austria is also a little late here; some countries have already paid, but unfortunately there are still some countries out there – and from the resolution of the value correction account in which Austria is also involved. First of all: A person's right to asylum, that is, to security from persecution in his home country, is a fundamental human right. The appeal to the so-called third-country security has become a pretext in recent years that asylum proceedings are generally refused in Austria. In a series of fundamental findings, the Administrative Court has proposed this practice. The Administrative Court has developed precise requirements for the assessment of third-country security: On the one hand, the premise of third-country security may, in accordance with the words of the Administrative Court of Justice, become "no entry for chain deportations", and on the other hand, according to the Court of Justice of the Administrative Court, the concept of "secure third country" also requires that the asylum procedure in that State fulfils minimum requirements. In doing so, the Verwaltungsgerichtshof has complied with the criteria of § 4 para. 3a Asylum Act, in which the legislator defined the legal requirements for third-country security. These legal provisions, however, now eliminate the government parties with the novel to the Asylum Act in place today. High house! The legal basis is thus to be removed from the jurisdiction of the Administrative Court of Justice. This is a blow to the face of a Supreme Court and at the same time an attack on human rights! Second comment: Mr Kiss and Mr Dr. Partik-Pablé have tabled an amendment in the Committee, in its current session, following a wide-ranging consultation procedure, on which the Director of Caritas Landau referred: “I’m outraged!”... Thus, according to this amendment, "States should already be considered safe from the law if they comply with the lowest standard of protection of an EU country. ‘This is a levelling down!’, Landau asks: What is the importance of the government's statements that Europe's harmonisation of protection standards should not mean lower levels if Austria itself – according to this proposal – is the lowest level in the EU? '" The call of the NGOs, the Caritas, many others, but also the opposition have led us to achieve that we have distanced ourselves from this draft. Thirdly, Mr President, I should like to thank the Ladies and gentlemen, has been declared in the Committee by Mr Strasser – I also read this in an APA issue –: "Innenminister Strasser stressed that the novelle was developed in close contact with the Verwaltungsgerichtshof." The quote here is in the APA message, the Minister is also correctly quoted, but it is not true, it is wrong, because I have asked the President of the Administrative Court, and he has confirmed to me today that no contact has been made with him and that what the Minister has said simply does not correspond to the truth. [[Abg. Silhavy: That's huge!]] This is just like the post closures, ladies and gentlemen: There are no posts closed, it's called, but: 118 have been closed or closed. In the same way, you also deal with human rights and asylum law. That's it. Thank you.
